-oriented flow category
\mathcal{F}
which lives over
L
.

Explicitly, this means that there are maps
\mathcal{F}_{xy}\to\Base(E)\times\mathcal{P}_{xy}L
covered by maps
(\mathcal{F}_{xy},I^{\mathcal{F}}_{xy})\to E\times\mathcal{P}L
where
(E\times\mathcal{P}L)(n):=E(n)\times\mathcal{P}L
and the vector bundle on the target is pulled back from
E(n)
.

There is a category
\Flow^{E}_{/L}
of
E
-oriented flow categories over
L
. Morphisms in this category are again given by bordism-over-
L
classes of
E
-oriented morphisms over
L
.

The construction of the unit morphism
\mathbb{D}\mathcal{F}
in Section
4.5
applies unchanged when
\mathcal{F}
lives over
L
, and makes
\Flow^{E}_{/L}
a unital category. Thus,
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
are equivalent in
\Flow^{E}_{/L}
exactly when there are
E
-oriented morphisms
\mathcal{W}:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}
over
L
for which the compositions
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
are bordant to the corresponding identity morphisms.

Definition 106
.

To define
*[i]
as an
E
-oriented flow cat over
L
, we choose a base-point
\ell_{0}\in L
, and specify that the point in
L
corresponding to the unique object of
*[i]
is
\ell_{0}
.

5
Floer theory and the Fukaya category without local systems

We quickly recap some relevant features of the spectral (Donaldson-)Fukaya category constructed in
[
PS24a
]
, beginning in the unoriented setting. We then incorporate tangential structures, importantly in a different way to
[
PS24b
]
and reflecting the fact that we now assume that our tangential structures are commutative. Let
X
be a graded Liouville domain of (real) dimension
2d
.

{rmk}

For the same tangential pair
\Psi
, the construction of the spectral Fukaya category we work with here differs from that of
[
PS24b
]
: the ‘
\Psi
-oriented flow categories’ of
loc. cit.
are expected to correspond to
R_{\Psi}
-
R_{\Psi}
bimodules (cf.
[
PS24b
, Conjecture 5.10]
), whereas those in this paper should correspond to
R_{\Psi}
-modules. The primary reason for the different set-ups is that in
[
PS24b
]
we did not impose any commutativity assumptions on
\Psi
.

Nevertheless we expect that the underlying Fukaya categories should be isomorphic to each other.

5.1
Unoriented version

Let
\Psi_{unor}=(BO\to BS_{\pm}U)
be the ‘universal’ choice of commutative tangential pair. In this section we define a Fukaya category corresponding to this commutative tangential pair, before moving onto the general case in Section
5.2
. We assume
X
is
graded
, meaning it is equipped with a lift of the classifying map of
TX\oplus\mathbb{C}^{N-d}
to
BS_{\pm}U(N)
, for some
N\gg 0
.

For a closed exact Lagrangian
L\subseteq X
, a
grading
on
L
is a homotopy between the two ways around the following diagram:

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{TL\oplus\mathbb{R}^{N-d}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{BO(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdot\otimes\mathbb{C}}
\textstyle{BS_{\pm}U(N)}

(5.1)

This is equivalent to the notion of grading from
[
Sei00
]
. The obstruction to existence of a grading is the
Maslov class
in
H^{2}(X,L)
.

We fix a finite set
\mathcal{L}
of closed exact graded Lagrangians in
X
. We call
\mathcal{L}
a choice of
Lagrangian data
. These will be the objects of the category
\mathcal{F}(X;\Psi_{unor})
.

For each pair of objects
L,K\in\mathcal{L}
, we choose regular Floer data
(H_{t},J_{t})
, which in particular satisfies
\phi_{H_{t}}^{1}(L)\pitchfork K
. We write
\mathcal{X}(L,K)
for the set of time-1 Hamiltonian chords from
L
to
K
, with respect to
H_{t}
, and for
x,y\in\mathcal{X}(L,K)
, we write
\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{xy}
for the space of (possibly broken) Floer trajectories from
x
to
y
, defined using the Floer data
(H_{t},J_{t})
. The grading data defines an integer
|x|\in\mathbb{Z}
for each
x\in Ob(\mathcal{M}^{LK})
(our grading conventions follow
[
PS24a
, Remark 1.4]
, i.e. homological grading with unit in degree zero).

{thmdef}

1.

Each
\mathcal{M}^{LK}
defines a(n unoriented) flow category.

The morphism group
\mathcal{F}_{i}(L,K;\Psi_{unor}):=\Omega_{i}(\mathcal{M}^{LK})
is defined to be the group of right modules of degree
i
over the Floer flow category
\mathcal{M}^{LK}
.

2.

For each triple
L,K,P\in\mathcal{L}
, there is a bilinear map
\mathcal{M}^{LKP}:\mathcal{M}^{KP}\times\mathcal{M}^{LK}\to\mathcal{M}^{LP}
.

Composition in
\mathcal{F}(X;\Psi_{unor})
is defined to be the bilinear map of groups
\mathcal{M}^{LKP}_{*}
induced by
\mathcal{M}^{LKP}
, as in Equation
4.5
.

3.

For each
L
, there is a distinguished element
\mathcal{E}^{L}\in\mathcal{F}(L,L;\Psi_{unor})
.

There is a bordism between the composition
\mathcal{E}^{L}\circ\mathcal{E}^{L}
and
\mathcal{E}^{L}
. Using this, it follows
[
PS24a
, Section 7.5]
that
\mathcal{E}^{L}
defines a unit for
L
in the category
\mathcal{F}(X;\Psi_{unor})
.

4.

For each quadruple
L,K,P,Q\in\mathcal{L}
, there is an associator
\mathcal{M}^{LKPQ}
, ensuring that composition in
\mathcal{F}(X;\Psi_{unor})
is associative.

The technical material that underlies this theorem is due to
[
Lar
,
FOOO24
]
, and the idea of constructing a category with morphism groups being bordism classes of flow modules is due to
[
AB24
]
.

Roughly, these are built from spaces of pseudoholomorphic curves as follows; see
[
PS24a
, Section 7]
for more detail (including a recap of the relevant parts of
[
Lar
,
FOOO24
]
):

1.

For
x,y\in\mathcal{X}(L,K)
, each
\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{xy}
is a moduli space of pseudoholomorphic discs with two punctures, one input and one output, and boundary on
L
and
K
.

2.

Each
\mathcal{M}^{LKP}_{xy;z}
is a moduli space of pseudoholomorphic discs with three punctures; two inputs are sent to
x
and
y
respectively, and the output to
z
. The boundary is required to lie on
L
,
K
and
P
.

3.

Each
\mathcal{E}^{L}_{x}
is a moduli space of discs with one output puncture sent to
x
, and boundary on
L
.

4.

Each
\mathcal{M}^{LKPQ}_{xyz;w}
is a moduli space of discs with four punctures; three are inputs sent to
x,y,z
and the output is sent to
w
.

{rmk}

In abuse of notation, we will write
\mathcal{M}^{LL}
for the flow category associated to the pair
(L,\phi_{f}^{1}(L))
where
f
is a
C^{2}
-small Morse function on
L
. Then
\mathcal{X}(L,L)
bijects with the critical points of the Morse function
f
.

{rmk}

Inputs and outputs are distinguished by whether one fixes incoming or outgoing strip-like ends, which in turn determines the asymptotics of the associated Floer data, see
[
Sei08
,
PS24a
,
PS24b
]
.

x
y
L
K
K
x
y
z
L
P

Figure 3:
Labelling conventions: an element of
\mathcal{M}^{LK}_{xy}
and of
\mathcal{M}^{LKP}_{xy;z}

{rmk}

Suppose we fix a spider on
L
. Then the category
\mathcal{M}^{LK}
naturally lives over
L
in the sense of Section
4.9
.

Along with the category
\mathcal{F}(X;\Psi_{unor})
, we also have a version of the open-closed map:

Theorem 107

(
[
PS24a
, Section 7.6]
)
.

1.

For each object
L\in\mathcal{L}
, there is a distinguished left module
\Upsilon_{L}\in\Omega^{d}(\mathcal{M}^{LL})
living over
X
.

2.

There is a bordism over
X
, from the composition
\Upsilon_{L}\circ\mathcal{E}^{L}
, which is a closed
d
-manifold lying over
X
, to
L
.

3.

For any pair
L,K\in\mathcal{L}
, there is a bilinear map
\mathcal{W}^{L}:\mathcal{M}^{LK}\times\mathcal{M}^{KL}\to\operatorname{pt}[d]
lying over
X
.

4.

There are bordisms of bilinear maps between
\Upsilon^{L}\circ\mathcal{M}^{LKL}
and
\mathcal{W}^{L}
, lying over
X
.

5.

For
L\neq K
, identifying
\mathcal{M}^{LK}\times\mathcal{M}^{KL}
and
\mathcal{M}^{KL}\times\mathcal{M}^{LK}
and recalling Remark
4.3
, the bilinear maps
\mathcal{W}^{L}
and
\mathcal{W}^{K}
are homotopic (and in particular, bordant) over
X
.

{rmk}

Similarly to Remark
5.1
, for appropriate choices of Floer data, the evaluation map from
\Upsilon_{L}
to
X
naturally factors through
L
, and so it defines a module over
L
. The same is true of the bordism in Theorem
107
(2).

Any object
L\in\mathcal{L}
tautologically determines a class
\iota^{L}_{*}[L]\in\Omega^{unor}_{d}(X)
in the unoriented bordism group of
X
.

Lemma 108
.

Let
L,K\in\mathcal{L}
be objects in
\scrF(X;\Psi_{unor})
. Assume they are isomorphic in
\scrF(X;\Psi_{unor})
.

Then
\iota^{L}_{*}[L]=\iota^{K}_{*}[K]\in\Omega^{unor}_{d}(X)
, i.e.
L
and
K
are bordant over
X
.

Proof 5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\in\scrF_{*}(L,K;\Psi_{unor})
and
\mathcal{B}\in\scrF_{*}(K,L;\Psi_{unor})
be a pair of inverse isomorphisms. This implies that
\mathcal{A}\circ\mathcal{B}
is bordant to
\mathcal{E}^{K}
, and
\mathcal{B}\circ\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{E}^{L}
.

By Theorem
107
(2),
L
is bordant to
\Upsilon_{L}\circ\mathcal{E}^{L}
over
X
, and hence to
\Upsilon_{L}\circ(\mathcal{B}\circ\mathcal{A})
. By Theorem
107
(4), this is bordant to
\mathcal{W}^{L}\circ(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
over
X
. By Theorem
107
(5), this is bordant to
\mathcal{W}^{K}\circ(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
over
X
; by the same argument as above, this is bordant to
K
over
X
.

5.2
Incorporating tangential pairs

Let
\Psi=(\Theta\to\Phi)
be a commutative tangential pair; recall this is a pair of commutative
\mathcal{I}
-spaces (satisfying a condition) sitting in a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\Theta\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{BO\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{BS_{\pm}U}

(5.2)

Let
E_{\Psi}
be the commutative Thom
\mathcal{I}
-monoid defined in Section
3.4
. Recall its base is equivalent to
\mathrm{hofib}(\Omega\Theta\to\Omega\Phi)
.

Definition 109
.

Let
X
be a Liouville domain. A
\Phi
-orientation

\phi
on
X
is a lift of the classifying map for
TX
, viewed as a stable complex vector bundle, to
\Phi
. Explicitly, this consists of a map
\phi:X\to\Phi(N)
for some
N\gg 0
, such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{TX\oplus\mathbb{C}^{N-n}}
\textstyle{\Phi(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{BU(N)}

(5.3)

Let
L\subseteq X
be a Lagrangian submanifold. A
\Theta
-orientation

\theta
on
L
is a homotopy lift of the classifying map for
TL
to
\Theta
, compatible with
\phi
. Explicitly, this consists of a map
\theta:L\to\Theta(N)
lifting the classifying map for
TL
(similarly to above for
X
and
\phi
), and which is compatible with
\phi
, meaning the following diagram commutes up to a given homotopy:

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\Theta(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(N)}

(5.4)

{rmk}

A
\Phi
-orientation on
X
(and a compatible
\Theta
-orientation on
L
) equip
X
(and
L
) with gradings in the sense of
[
Sei00
]
, as in Section
5.1
.

{asmp}

We assume
X
is equipped with a
\Phi
-structure
\phi
, and that all objects
L\in\mathcal{L}
are equipped with compatible
\Theta
-structures.

Definition 110
.

Let
\phi
be a
\Phi
-structure on
X
. Let
\widetilde{X}
be the homotopy pullback of the maps
X,\Theta(N)\to\Phi(N)
; this maps to
\Theta(N)
and hence to
BO(N)
. Let
T\to\widetilde{X}
be the pullback of the tautological bundle over
BO(N)
. A
E_{\Psi}
-orientation relative to
\phi
manifold/left module/bilinear map/bordism lying over
X
is a
E_{\Psi}
-orientation relative to
T
and lying over
\widetilde{X}
(lifting the maps to
X
), in the sense of Section
4.4
.

Theorem 111
.

Under Assumption
5.2
:

The flow categories, bilinear maps, units and associators considered in Theorem
5.1
all compatibly admit
E_{\Psi}
-orientations. Furthermore, these
E_{\Psi}
-orientations are canonical up to concordance in the sense of Example
4.2
.

Similarly, the left modules, bilinear maps and bordisms considered in Theorem
107
are compatibly
E_{\Psi}
-oriented relative to the
\Phi
-orientation
\phi
on
X
, and (in the case of (2)) the natural
E_{\Psi}
-orientation on
L
relative to
\phi
.

We defer the proof of Theorem
111
to Section
5.4
.

Definition 112
.

We define the category
\mathcal{F}(X;\Psi)
to have:

•

Objects are elements
L\in\mathcal{L}
.

Recall this consists of some chosen collection of closed exact Lagrangians
L\subseteq X
, equipped with
\Theta
-orientations, compatible with
\phi
, the chosen
\Phi
-orientation on
X
.

•

Morphisms from
L
to
K
are the right bordism groups
\mathcal{F}_{*}(L,K;\Psi):=\Omega_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}^{LK})
.

•

Composition is induced by the
E
-oriented bilinear maps
\mathcal{M}^{LKP}
.

•

The units are the
E
-oriented right modules
\mathcal{E}^{L}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(L,L;\Psi)
.

Definition 113
.

For
i\geq 0
, we define the
\tau_{\leq i}\scrF(X;\Psi)
to be the category with objects the same as
\scrF(X;\Psi)
, and morphisms
L
to
K
given by
\Omega^{E_{\Psi},\leq i}_{*}(\tau_{\leq i}\mathcal{M}^{LK})
, the
i
-truncated (bordism classes of) left module over the truncated Floer flow category
\tau_{\leq i}\mathcal{M}^{LK}
.

Composition is defined using the truncation of the usual Floer bilinear maps.

5.3
Open-closed map with tangential pairs

Let
\Psi=(\Theta\to\Phi)
be a commutative tangential pair,
F=\mathrm{hofib}(\Theta\to\Phi)
the homotopy fibre and
E_{\Psi}
the commutative Thom
\mathcal{I}
-monoid constructed in Section
3.2
. Let
X
be a Liouville domain equipped with a
\Phi
-orientation
\phi:X\to\Phi(N)
.

Let
T\to\widetilde{X}
be as in Definition
110
. We write
\Omega^{E_{\Psi},\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{i}(X,\phi)
for the groups
\Omega^{E_{\Psi}}_{*}(\widetilde{X},T)
. Note that for
N\gg 0
this is independent of
N
: more precisely, for fixed
i
and sufficiently large
N\gg 0
, letting
\phi^{\prime}
be the composition of
\phi
with
\Phi(N)\to\Phi(N+1)
,
\Omega^{E_{\Psi},\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{i}(X,\phi)\simeq\Omega^{E_{\Psi},\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{i}(X,\phi^{\prime})
.

For a
\Psi
-oriented Lagrangian
L\subseteq X
, we define
\Omega^{E_{\Psi},\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{*}(L,\phi|_{L})
similarly using the restriction of
\phi
to
L
.

{rmk}

The groups
\Omega^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{i}(X,\phi)
are naturally isomorphic to the set of cobordism classes of tuples
(M,f,k,\rho,h)
where:

•

f:M\to X
is a closed
i
-manifold over
X
.

•

k\in\mathcal{I}
.

•

\rho:(M,TM)(k+N)\to(\Theta(N),\Gamma)\times E(i+k)
is a map of Thom spaces, where
\Gamma\to\Theta(N)
is the pullback of the universal bundle over
BO(N)
.

•

h
is a homotopy between the two maps
M\to\Phi(N)
which factor through
f
and
\Base(\rho)
respectively.

The definition of the groups
\Omega_{*}^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}(\bullet)
makes sense for any pair
(Y,\phi)
where
Y
is a cell complex with a complex vector bundle
\phi:Y\to\Phi\to BU
equipped with a
\Phi
-lift
\phi
. Recall that
the ring spectrum
R_{\Psi}
has an associated generalised cohomology theory
R^{*}(\cdot)
.

Lemma 114
.

The groups
\Omega_{*}^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}(Y,\phi)
are modules over
R_{\Psi}^{-*}(Y)
.

Proof 5.2

(Proof sketch)
.

Let
f:M\to Y
be as above. Let
\alpha\in R^{-i}(Y)
; represent this my a map
Y\to\Omega^{\infty+i}R
. This lifts to a map
Y\to\Omega^{j+i}\Thom(E(j))
for some
j\gg 0
.

Considering the composition
Y\to\Omega^{j+i}\Thom(E(j))
and taking adjoints, we obtain a map
M_{+}\wedge S^{j+i}\to\Thom(E(j))
. Generically perturbing and taking the preimage of the zero-section defines a new
(j+i)
-dimensional manifold
M^{\prime}
; it has a natural map to
Y
along with the relevant tangential structures to determine a class in
\Omega^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{*}(Y,\phi)
. We leave to the reader that this gives a well-defined and linear action.

Lemma 115
.

Let
L\in\mathcal{L}
be a
\Theta
-oriented Lagrangian in
X
. Then
L
admits a natural
E_{\Psi}
-orientation relative to
\phi|_{L}
, and hence defines a class
[L]\in\Omega^{E_{\Psi},\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{d}(L;\phi|_{L})
.

Pushing forwards along the inclusion defines
\iota^{L}:L\to X
then defines a class
\iota^{L}_{*}[L]\in\Omega^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{d}(X;\phi)
.

Proof 5.3
.

This is essentially by the second part of Definition
109
: the
E_{\Psi}
-orientation can be taken to be trivial.

Lemma 116
.

Let
g:M\to L
be a map from a closed manifold, which is
E_{\Psi}
-oriented relative to
\phi|_{L}
. Let
f:M\to\Omega^{n}\Thom(\mathbb{V}^{\Psi}(n)\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(n))=\Omega^{\infty}R_{\Psi}(n)
for some
n
, and
\hat{f}:M\times S^{n}\to\Thom(\mathbb{V}^{\Psi}(n)\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(n))
a generic perturbation of its adjoint. Let
\hat{M}
be the preimage of 0; note this is also naturally
E_{\Psi}
-oriented relative to
\phi|_{L}
.

Let
[f]\in R_{\Psi}^{0}(M)
be the class represented by the composition
M\to\Omega^{\infty}R_{\Psi}(n)\to\Omega^{\infty}R_{\Psi}
. Then:

g_{*}[\hat{M}]=g_{*}\left([M]\cap[f]\right)\in\Omega^{E_{\Psi},\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{d}(L,\phi|_{L})

(5.5)

Proof 5.4
.

[f]\in R_{\Psi}^{0}(M)
is Atiyah dual to a class in
\Omega^{E_{\Psi}}_{d}(M;-TM)
; chasing through the Pontrjagin-Thom construction, this class is represented by
\hat{M}
. In terms of the cap product, the Atiyah dual class is
[M]\cap[f]
. Pushing forwards along
g_{*}
gives the desired formula.

Proposition 117
.

Assume Assumption
5.2
. Let
L,K\in\mathcal{L}
be objects in
\scrF(X;\Psi)
.

If
L
and
K
are isomorphic, then
\iota^{L}_{*}[L]=\iota^{K}_{*}[K]\in\Omega^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{d}(X;\phi)
.

In words:
L
and
K
are
E_{\Psi}
-oriented bordant over
X
, relative to
\phi
.

Proof 5.5
.

Follows from the same argument as Lemma
108
, now using the given
E_{\Psi}
-orientations from Theorem
111
.

{rmk}

[Relation to constructions in
[
PS24b
]
]

In
[
PS24b
]
, associated to any graded Liouville domain
X
with
\Phi
-structure and any set of Lagrangians
\mathcal{L}
with compatible
\Theta
-structures, we also constructed a Fukaya category with objects
\mathcal{L}
. Here our construction differs: our notion of
E
-oriented flow category considered here is stronger than the definition considered in
[
PS24b
, Section 5]
.
In particular, we are able to bypass the use of “capsule bordism” as the target of the open-closed map considered in
[
PS24a
, Section 5.10]
– morally this is an analogue of the splitting of ring spectra
THH(R)\to R
for a commutative Thom ring spectrum
R

[
Blu10
]
, which fails to hold in general if
R
is not commutative.

The key difference is that in
[
PS24b
]
, we worked in a more general setting, where we did not assume that
\Theta
and
\Phi
were commutative. In this paper, the results of Section
8
require strong commutativity assumptions.

{ex}

In specific cases, the bordism theory
\Omega_{*}^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}
maps to a more familiar theory, even if it does not obviously co-incide with anything classical:

1.

If
\Phi=\{pt\}
(so
X
is stably framed) then there is a map
\Omega_{*}^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}(X,\phi)\to\Omega_{*}^{E_{\Psi}}(X)
. Taking
\Theta
the pullback of
\widetilde{U/O}\to U/O
under
U\to U/O
, there is a map
\Omega^{E_{\Psi}}_{*}(X)\to MU_{*}(X)
.

2.

If
\Psi=(pt,BS_{\pm}U)
(so the Lagrangians are stably framed) then again there is a map
\Omega^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}(X,\phi)\to MU_{*}(X)
.

3.

If
(\Theta\to\Phi)=(BO\times F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}BO)
, then
X
is polarised, i.e.
TX\sim V\otimes\mathbb{C}
is stably a complexification. There is a map
\Omega^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}(X,\phi)\to\Omega^{E_{\Psi}}_{*}(X,V)=\Omega^{E_{\Psi}}_{*}(\Thom(-V))
to the bordism theory of manifolds
f:M\to X
with an
E_{\Psi}
-orientation of
TM-f^{*}V
. In the special case
F=B(G/O)
where
G=GL_{1}(\mathbb{S})
, then
\Omega^{E_{\Psi}}_{*}=\Omega^{\mathbb{S}}_{*}
is bordism of manifolds with a trivialisation of the stable tangent spherical fibration.

4.

The Unoriented case
\Psi=BO\to BU
. In this case the data of the homotopy
h
in Remark
5.3
means that
\Omega^{E_{\Psi};\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}}_{*}(X,\phi)
is the bordism of pairs of a manifold
f:M\to X
equipped with a stable polarisation of
f^{*}TX
. This admits a forgetful map to
MO_{*}(X)
.

5.4
Proof of Theorem
111

Proof 5.6
.

We prove Theorem
111
in stages.

Single moduli space, 1 output

We begin by constructing a
E_{\Psi}
-orientation on a single Floer moduli space
\mathcal{M}
with 1 output (so one of the moduli spaces considered in Theorem
5.1
).
Let
i
be the number of inputs of
\mathcal{M}
. We assume we have chosen index data
d_{\mathcal{M}}\in\mathcal{I}
, as in Definition
85
. We wish to construct a map of Thom spaces

(\mathcal{M},I^{\mathcal{M}})(\nu_{\mathcal{M}})\to(\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}[l](\nu_{\mathcal{M}}+d_{\mathcal{M}}),\mathbb{V})

(5.6)

for some
\nu_{\mathcal{M}}\in\mathcal{I}
. Here
l=0
for Floer moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}
arising in Theorem
5.1
(1),
l=1
if in Theorem
5.1
(2) or (3), and
l=2
if in Theorem
5.1
(4).

Set-up from
[
PS24b
]

There is a choice of framed puncture data
\mathbb{E}_{x}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{L})
with Maslov index equal to the grading of
|x|
, cf.
[
PS24b
, Section 6]
. We write
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}
for the tuple of puncture data given by those of the inputs and outputs of
\mathcal{M}
.

The main result of
[
PS24b
, Section 6]
is that for
N,\nu_{\mathcal{M}}\gg 0
sufficiently large, there is a map of Thom spaces:

\rho^{orig}_{\mathcal{M}}:(\mathcal{M},I^{\mathcal{M}})(\nu_{\mathcal{M}})\to(\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}}[l](\nu_{\mathcal{M}},N),\mathbb{V})

(5.7)

Outline of strategy

We begin by separating the given map
\rho^{orig}_{\mathcal{M}}
into two pieces: roughly speaking, this is a lift
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
of
\rho^{orig}_{\mathcal{M}}
along a map of the form
\oplus:\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{\ldots}(\ldots)\times_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{S}_{i1}}\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{\ldots}(\ldots)\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\ldots)
.

Next, we trivialise the index bundle over the image in the first factor
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
(which we may do since it is a space of framed discs). In the second factor, we choose a “capping map”
\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{01}(0,\ldots)
(note the index bundle over the target is 0) and glue this to the inputs of the second factor
\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
. Together, this defines a map of Thom spaces
(\mathcal{M},I^{\mathcal{M}})\to(\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\ldots),\mathbb{V})\oplus\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{M}}}
. Lifting along the (highly-connected) inclusion
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(\ldots)\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\ldots)\oplus\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{M}}}
(cf. Remark
3.4
) provides the desired
\mathbb{E}_{\Psi}
-orientation on
\mathcal{M}
.

Separating into two pieces

We choose
N^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}\gg 0
large, and injections

\textstyle{[N^{\prime}]\sqcup[\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[N]}
and
\textstyle{[\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}]\sqcup[\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\nu_{\mathcal{M}}]}

(5.8)

We may assume that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}=\mathbb{E}^{std}_{i1}\oplus\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
, for some other framed puncture data
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Consider the following homotopy lifting problem:

\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},N^{\prime})\times_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{S}_{i1}}\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{i1}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\nu_{\mathcal{M}},N)}

(5.9)

The right vertical map is induced by the maps (
5.8
), and is highly connected (more precisely: for sufficiently large choices of
N^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
, its connectivity is larger than the dimension of
\mathcal{M}
), so we may choose such a homotopy lift. We write
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{E}^{fr}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},N^{\prime})
and
\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{i1}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
for the two factors. The homotopy lift
(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
extends naturally to one of Thom spaces; explicitly, after including the fibre product into the product, we obtain a map of Thom spaces:

(\mathcal{M},I^{\mathcal{M}})(\nu_{\mathcal{M}})\to\left(\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},N^{\prime})\times\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{i1}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}),\mathbb{V}\oplus\mathbb{V}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{\mathcal{M}}-\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}-\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{N-N^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{l}\right)

(5.10)

Note that we do not treat the summands
(\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\ldots),\mathbb{V})
and
(\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{i1}(\ldots),\mathbb{V})
on the RHS separately: in particular, we do not separate
I^{\mathcal{M}}
into two summands, and then lift them separately. We adopt the same convention in future lifting arguments without comment.

The pieces

\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},N^{\prime})
is highly connected, so we may trivialise the index bundle
\mathbb{V}
over the image of
\mathcal{M}
; note its rank is
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}+d_{\mathcal{M}}
.

\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
lands in a space of abstract discs with
i
inputs; our goal (
5.6
) is to land in a space with none. To deal with this, we glue “caps” to these inputs. Explicitly, choose a map
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathcal{M}\to(\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{01}(0,\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}))^{i}
.
The target is not in general contractible (even as
\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}\to\infty
), but by Lemma
79
it contains a contractible subspace; enforcing that
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}
lands in this subspace thus specifies
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}
up to contractible choice. Also note the index bundle of the codomain of
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}
is 0. Now compose
\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
with the map which glues these discs on, and we obtain a map
\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
; since the index bundle over the space of caps used here is 0, this naturally extends to a map of Thom spaces.

The inclusion
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
is highly-connected, so we may lift
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
along it. Combining with the trivialisation of the framed part above, we obtain a map of Thom spaces
(\mathcal{M},I^{\mathcal{M}})(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}[l](\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{M}}}
, which we then may compose with the map
E_{\Psi}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{M}}}\to E_{\Psi}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}+d_{\mathcal{M}})
to obtain (
5.6
).

We next explain how to modify the above argument so that the resulting
E_{\Psi}
orientations are compatible with Floer gluing.

Coherent combinatorics

We choose
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
each as part of some choice of stabilisation data in the sense of Section
4.2
. We may additionally choose the maps (
5.8
) to be compatible with the injections which are part of the stabilisation data.

We may assume the choices of puncture data are compatible with this data, namely that for any inclusion of a boundary face
\prod_{l}\mathcal{A}_{l}\to\mathcal{M}
(here
\mathcal{A}_{l}
,
\mathcal{M}
are moduli spaces appearing in the statement of the theorem),
\oplus_{l}\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}_{l}}
stabilises to
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
. See Section
118
for further discussion. Recall that each such moduli space is a manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by products of other such moduli spaces of lower dimensions.

Coherent lifts

It is proved in
[
PS24b
, Section 6]
that the maps
\rho^{orig}_{\mathcal{M}}
are compatible with inclusions of boundary faces on the LHS of (
5.7
) and with gluing of abstract discs on the RHS of (
5.7
).

The right hand column of (
5.9
) is also compatible with inclusions of boundary faces (as a diagram of Thom spaces). Note that the gluing operation on the top right entry of (
5.9
) comes from concatenation in the two factors separately, along with appropriate stabilisation. By choosing the homotopy lift inductively over
\dim(\mathcal{M})
, we may therefore assume the homotopy lifts
(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
are also compatible with inclusions of boundary faces.

Since all
\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
are highly connected, by choosing them inductively over
\dim(\mathcal{M})
, we may trivialise their index bundles over the images of each
\mathcal{M}
coherently with respect to inclusions of boundary faces.

Now let
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{P}
be an inclusion of a boundary faces.
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{P}}
is not directly related to
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{N}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{N}}
;

Note that
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{N}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{N}}
can’t be
\oplus
ed together over
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}
, since they don’t land in a space of abstract discs with the same domain. The inclusion of the subspace with the same domains
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\ldots)\times_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{S}_{01}}\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\ldots)\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\ldots)\times\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\ldots)
is highly connected, and so a homotopy lift of
(\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\gamma_{\mathcal{N}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{N}})
exists, and in fact
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{P}}
is obtained from
(\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\gamma_{\mathcal{N}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{N}})
by choosing some homotopy lift (of maps of Thom spaces), and then direct summing. The space of choices in how one does this is highly connected (note that the contractible space of choices of the capping maps is incorporated into this). A more restrictive (but still highly-connected in terms of the space of choices) way to do this is to choose homotopy lifts of the
E_{\Psi}
-orientations of
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{N}
along the inclusion
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(\ldots)\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\ldots)
. Hence the stabilisation of
E_{\Psi}
-orientation on
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}
is homotopic to the restriction of that of
\mathcal{P}
.

Using this, over a collar neighbourhood of the face
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}
in
\mathcal{P}
, we may homotope the
E_{\psi}
-orientation on
\mathcal{P}
so that these agree on the nose.

The same argument applies to more general inclusions of boundary faces
\prod_{l}\mathcal{A}_{l}\to\mathcal{P}
. By first inducting over
\dim(\mathcal{P})
and then inducting over the dimension of the faces of
\mathcal{P}
, we may homotope the
E_{\Psi}
-orientation on
\mathcal{P}
so that it agrees with the direct sums of the
E_{\Psi}
-orientations on its faces induced by the direct sums of those of the lower-dimensional moduli spaces; this is exactly the required coherence.

0 output case: set-up

Now assume
\mathcal{M}
is a moduli space of pseudohomolorphic curves with no outputs, i.e. one arising in the statement of Theorem
107
. Then instead of (
5.7
),
[
PS24b
, Section 6]
produces a map of Thom spaces:

\rho^{orig}_{\mathcal{M}}:(\mathcal{M},I^{\mathcal{M}})(\nu_{\mathcal{M}}+N)\to(\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}}[l](\nu_{\mathcal{M}},N),\mathbb{V})

(5.11)

Furthermore, there are maps of spaces
\varepsilon:\mathcal{M}\to X
, such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}}[l](\nu_{\mathcal{M}},N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(N)}

(5.12)

Both horizontal maps are given by evaluating at the interior marked point.

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}
is defined similarly to before, and
l=1
if we are considering a moduli space from Theorem
107
(1) or (3), and
l=2
otherwise. Recall from Definition
68
that when
\mathcal{M}
has no outputs (as is the case currently), elements of
\mathbb{U}_{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}}}^{\Psi}(\ldots)
are equipped with a marked point
p
in the interior of the domain Riemann surface, along with a tangent direction at
p
, pointing towards some point on (the smoothing of) the boundary of the domain Riemann surface.

0 outputs: lifting

We make choices (
5.8
) as before, and “separate into two pieces” by choosing a homotopy lift analogously to (
5.9
), but with
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{i0}(\ldots)
in the top right of the diagram instead of
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{i1}(\ldots)
. We now have a map
(\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}):\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{U}^{fr}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}(\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},N^{\prime})\times_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{S}_{i0}}\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{i0}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
, along with an extension to a map of Thom spaces.

We may trivialise the index bundle over the image of
\rho^{\prime}
similarly to before; note this has rank
d_{\mathcal{M}}+\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}+N^{\prime}
. We may choose a capping map
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}
as before to obtain
\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{00}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
(and an extension to a Thom map). At this stage, we must deviate slightly from the earlier construction.

We define two new types of space of abstract discs. These are variants of Definition
71
:

•

\mathbb{U}^{\Psi,fr}_{0*}(v,n)\subseteq\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{00}(v,n)
is defined to be the space of abstract discs with 0 inputs and 0 outputs, such that if
p
is the point in the domain Riemann surface which the asymptotic marker points to, the boundary conditions are trivialised over
p
(i.e. the homotopy lift to
\Theta
over
p
is lifted further to
fr
).

•

\mathbb{U}^{const,\Theta}_{00}(v,n)\subseteq\mathbb{U}^{(\Theta,\Theta)}_{00}(v,n)
is the subspace where the map from the domain Riemann surface to
\Theta
is constant.

Here
(\Theta,\Theta)
is the commutative tangential pair where both spaces are given by
\Theta
.

There are maps
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi,fr}_{0*}(v,n)\to\Omega F(n)
and
\mathbb{U}^{const,\Theta}_{00}(v,n)\to\Theta(n)
whose connectivity go to infinity as
v\to\infty
. Both of these spaces naturally map also to
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{00}(v,n)
, and are equipped with the pullback of the index bundle from it. Note that by the constancy condition, the index bundle over
\mathbb{U}^{const,\Theta}_{00}(v,n)
is isomorphic to the bundle pulled back along the composition to
\Theta(n)\to BO(n)
.

Recall that
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{00}(\ldots)
approximates the homotopy limit of the diagram given by the two maps
\mathcal{L}\Theta_{h\mathcal{I}},\Phi_{h\mathcal{I}}\to\mathcal{L}\Phi_{h\mathcal{I}}
; since these are infinite loop spaces, this is equivalent to
\Omega F_{h\mathcal{I}}\times\Theta_{h\mathcal{I}}
, the product of the subspaces defined above.

We choose
\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}\gg 0\in\mathcal{I}
and an injection
[\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}]\sqcup[\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}]\to[\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}]
, and consider the homotopy lifting problem:

\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{\Psi,fr}_{0*}(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\times_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{S}_{00}}\mathbb{U}^{const,\Theta}_{00}(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\#\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{00}(\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\nu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})}

(5.13)

By the arguments above, the vertical map is highly connected, so we may choose a homotopy lift; write
(\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
for it. We compose
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
with the map
\mathbb{U}^{const,\Theta}_{00}(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\to\Theta(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
.

Now choose some framed “co-cap”
C\in\mathbb{U}^{fr,fr}_{1*}(0,\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
; there is a contractible space of such choices as before. We choose a homotopy lift:

\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdot\#C}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{\Psi,fr}_{0*}(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})}

(5.14)

We then choose a further homotopy lift along the (highly-connected) inclusion
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}_{01}(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
.

Combining the above, we have constructed a map
\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\times\Theta(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
, which may be extended to a map of Thom spaces; we call this
\rho_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Relativity to
\phi

Evaluation at the interior marked point determines maps
\mathbb{U}^{\Psi,fr}_{0*}(v,n)\to\Phi(n)
and
\mathbb{U}^{const,\Theta}_{00}(v,n)\to\Theta(n)\to\Phi(n)
. The first of these is nullhomotopic, via a nullhomotopy given by evaluating along the path from the interior marked point to the boundary marked point (whose evaluation to
\Phi(n)
is trivial by definition).

Since each of the lifts in the above steps are compatible with maps to
\Phi(\cdot)
, we find that the composition
\mathcal{M}\to X\to\Phi(N)
factors through the map
(\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})
. By the above observation, we then have that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\Phi(N)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{U}^{\Psi}(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\times\Theta(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Theta(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{M}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Theta(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(5.15)

so
\rho_{\mathcal{M}}
is exactly the data of a
E_{\Psi}
-orientation relative to
\phi
.

The intermediate choices can be made so that the resulting
E_{\Psi}
-orientations relative to
\phi
are compatible with boundary breaking of moduli spaces.

6
Floer moduli spaces and their combinatorics

In later sections, it will be convenient to keep track of the collection of all moduli spaces considered in Section
5
.

6.1
Abstract moduli spaces and gluing

Definition 118
.

Assume we have fixed a choice of Lagrangian data
\mathcal{L}
.

The set of
Floer moduli spaces (with respect to
\mathcal{L}
)
is defined to be the set of all moduli spaces considered in Theorems
5.1
and
107
, with boundary conditions given by Lagrangians in
\mathcal{L}
.

Each Floer moduli space is a manifold with faces, with a system of faces given by products of other Floer moduli spaces. Let
f:\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{A}_{k}\to\mathcal{M}
be an inclusion of a face, from a product of Floer moduli spaces into another Floer moduli space. We call such a map
f
a
Floer gluing
.

We call a Floer gluing which is of the form
\mathcal{A}_{1}\to\mathcal{M}
(i.e.
k=1
) and codimension 1 an
unbroken Floer gluing
. We call a Floer gluing which is of the form
\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2}\to\mathcal{M}
and codimension 1 a
simple (broken) Floer gluing
.

{rmk}

Any Floer gluing
\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{A}_{k}\to\mathcal{M}
is a composition of products of unbroken and simple unbroken Floer gluings. In particular, it will often be sufficient to check various associativity relations on Floer gluings just of this form.

{rmk}

The definitions in Section
4
along with Theorems
5.1
and
107
enforce various associativity relations on Floer gluing maps; essentially, whenever one can write down a square of Floer gluing maps, it commutes. More explicitly:

•

Let
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{Q}
,
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{R}
,
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{S}
be Floer gluings. Then the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}}

(6.1)

•

Let
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{P}
,
\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{P}
be Floer gluings. Then the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}}

(6.2)

A similar compatibility holds when
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{N}
are swapped.

•

Let
\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
,
\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{P}
be Floer gluings. Then the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}}

(6.3)

By Remark
6.1
, similar associativity relations hold for all Floer gluings of the form
\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{A}_{k}\to\mathcal{M}
, but they are all implied by the ones above.

We assumed
\mathcal{L}
was finite. Since for each type of structure considered in Theorems
5.1
and
107
there are only finitely many Floer moduli spaces for each pair/triple/quadruple of Lagrangians, we see that:

Lemma 119
.

Assume
\mathcal{L}
is finite. Then the collection of Floer moduli spaces is finite.

In particular, there exists some
n^{max}\gg 0
such that all Floer moduli spaces have dimension
\leq n^{max}
.

6.2
Boundary labelling data

Definition 120
.

An
\mathcal{L}
-labelled domain

\Delta
consists of the following data:

•

Integers
0\leq i\leq 3
and
0\leq j\leq 1
.

•

A set of
i
“input” marked points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}
and
j
“output” marked points (called
x_{0}
if
j=1
) on the boundary of the disc
\partial D^{2}
.

•

A labelling of each boundary component
b
of
D^{2}\setminus\{x_{l}\}_{l}
with an object
L\in\mathcal{L}
.

We write
\operatorname{Cpt}\partial\Delta
for the set of such boundary components.

•

A labelling of each
x_{l}
with an object (that we also call
x_{l}
) of the flow category
\mathcal{M}^{L_{b},L_{b^{\prime}}}
, where
b,b^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Cpt}\partial\Delta
are the two boundary components touching
x_{l}
.

Here
b
and
b^{\prime}
are ordered according to the following convention. If
x_{l}
is an input,
b^{\prime}
is clockwise of
x_{l}
(and
b
is anticlockwise of
x_{l}
), and the opposite way around if
x_{l}
is an output (i.e.
l=0
).

We require that the inputs
x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}
are ordered anticlockwise, so that if
j=1
, the output lies directly between
x_{i}
and
x_{1}
.

We consider two
\mathcal{L}
-labelled domains as the same if there is an oriented homeomorphism of the disc, relating the two
\mathcal{L}
-labellings.

We write
\mathcal{D}_{ij}
for the set of
\mathcal{L}
-labelled boundary data with
i
inputs and
j
outputs.

Definition 121
.

Any Floer moduli space
\mathcal{M}
determines a
\mathcal{L}
-labelled domain
\Delta_{\mathcal{M}}
as follows. The interior of
\mathcal{M}
is defined to be the space of maps from a punctured disc
D
with boundary punctures to
X
satisfying a PDE (a perturbed Cauchy-Riemann equation) with Lagrangian boundary conditions, and which are required to asymptote to some fixed Hamiltonian chord at each puncture. Choose a homeomorphism between
D
and a punctured copy of
D^{2}
. The boundary conditions for the PDE determine, for each component
b
of
D
, an object
L\in\mathcal{L}
. The asymptotic conditions at each puncture of
D
determine some
x\in\mathcal{M}^{L_{b},L_{b^{\prime}}}
, where
b
and
b^{\prime}
are the boundary components neighbouring
x
, ordered as in Definition
120
.

For any product
\mathcal{F}=\prod_{l}\mathcal{A}_{l}
of Floer moduli spaces, we set
\operatorname{Cpt}\partial\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}=\sqcup_{l}\operatorname{Cpt}\partial\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{l}}
.

\mathcal{M}\ni
y

z

\small{L_{2}}

for a moduli space
\mathcal{M}
.

An example is shown in Figure
4
.

{rmk}

Note that even though as a Riemann surface the domain can vary over the moduli space, the isomorphism class of
\mathcal{L}
-labelled boundary data does not.

{rmk}

Each
\Delta_{\mathcal{M}}
lies in
\mathcal{D}_{ij}
, where
i
is the number of inputs of the domain of
\mathcal{M}
and
j
is the number of outputs.

{rmk}

For a simple Floer gluing
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{P}
, the output of (WLOG)
\mathcal{M}
is glued to the
l^{th}
input of
\mathcal{N}
(with respect to the anticlockwise ordering of the inputs) for some
l
. We say
l
is the
gluing number
of this Floer gluing.

We define the set of
Floer generators

\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{L})
to be the disjoint union
\sqcup_{L,K\in\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{X}(L,K)
.

Definition 122
.

There are maps
s_{l}:\mathcal{D}_{ij}\to\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{L})
where
s_{l}
sends
\Delta
to its
l^{\textrm{th}}
input. If
j=1
, there is a similar map
t:\mathcal{D}_{i1}\to\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{L})
sending
\Delta
to its output.

For
1\leq l\leq i
and
i+i^{\prime}-1\leq 3
, there are maps of sets:

\lambda_{l}:\mathcal{D}_{i1}\prescript{}{t}{\times}_{s_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{i^{\prime}j}\to\mathcal{D}_{i+i^{\prime}-1,j}

(6.4)

where we take the fibre product over
\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{L})
. This is defined by gluing two labelled discs
\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}
together, by gluing the output of
\Delta
to the
l^{\mathrm{th}}
input of
\Delta^{\prime}
. These are suitably associative, in the sense that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{i1}\prescript{}{t}{\times}_{s_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{i^{\prime}1}\prescript{}{t}{\times}_{s_{l}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}_{i^{\prime}j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{i1}\prescript{}{t}{\times}_{s_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{i^{\prime}+i^{\prime\prime}-1,j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{i+i^{\prime}-1,1}\prescript{}{t}{\times}_{s_{l^{\prime}}}\mathcal{D}_{i^{\prime\prime}j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{i+i^{\prime}+i^{\prime\prime}-2,j}}

(6.5)

We call pairs
(\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})
which we can apply
\lambda_{l}
to (i.e. they lie in
\mathcal{D}_{i1}\prescript{}{t}{\times}_{s_{l}}\mathcal{D}_{i^{\prime}j}
, and

.

. By convention, outputs are on the right.

{rmk}

Let
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{P}
be a simple Floer gluing of gluing number
l
. Then
\Delta_{\mathcal{M}}
and
\Delta_{\mathcal{N}}
are
l
